//! Conservative transitive write closure for pre-write policy snapshots.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#127-stable-judgments-and-direct-payload-witnesses — implicit writes cannot establish pre-write intent.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn implicit(bound:&Bound,p:&Program)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut affected=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().map(|(t,_)|t).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    // A plain update cannot trigger an on-delete cascade. Maintains, live
    // read-through retirement and expiring tables can still remove rows.
    let mut removed=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter(|(t,e)|*e=="delete"||bound.schema.tables.get(t).is_some_and(|t|t.is_expiring())).map(|(t,_)|t).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let mut implicit=BTreeSet::new();
    loop{
        let before=(affected.len(),removed.len());
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains{if affected.contains(&m.source)||m.row_key.as_ref().is_some_and(|r|affected.contains(&r.table)){
            if bound.schema.table(&m.target).is_some(){implicit.insert(m.target.clone());affected.insert(m.target.clone());removed.insert(m.target.clone());}
            if let Some(owner)=&m.private{implicit.insert(owner.table.clone());affected.insert(owner.table.clone());removed.insert(owner.table.clone());}
        }}
        for (name,t) in &bound.schema.tables{
            if t.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|edge|edge.live.is_some()&&affected.contains(&edge.parent)){implicit.insert(name.clone());affected.insert(name.clone());removed.insert(name.clone());}
            for (column,action) in &t.on_delete{
            if action.writes()&&let Some(C::Ref{table})=t.columns.get(column).map(C::required)&&removed.contains(table){implicit.insert(name.clone());affected.insert(name.clone());if matches!(action,snapback4_core::schema::references::OnDelete::Cascade)||t.is_expiring(){removed.insert(name.clone());}}
        }}
        if before==(affected.len(),removed.len()){break;}
    }implicit
}

pub(super) fn dependencies(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr)->BTreeSet<String>{
    let mut deps=kernel::dependencies(rule);
    loop{let before=deps.len();for name in deps.clone(){if let Some(t)=bound.schema.table(&name){deps.extend(kernel::dependencies(&t.rules.read));if let Some(edge)=&t.through{deps.insert(edge.parent.clone());}}}if before==deps.len(){return deps;}}
}
/// A stable necessary condition may refute an intent even when another
/// conjunct changes. Dropping a requirement never proves the full policy.
pub(super) fn necessary(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,inv:&Invariant)->Option<Invariant>{
    let policy=inv.policy.as_ref()?;let implicit=implicit(bound,p);
    if implicit.contains(&inv.table){return None;}
    let mut changed=implicit;
    changed.extend(ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter(|(t,_)|t!=&inv.table).map(|(t,_)|t));
    fn weak(bound:&Bound,r:&RuleExpr,changed:&BTreeSet<String>)->RuleExpr{
        match r{
            RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|weak(bound,r,changed)).collect()),
            RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|weak(bound,r,changed)).collect()),
            _ if dependencies(bound,r).iter().any(|d|changed.contains(d))=>RuleExpr::Allow,
            _=>r.clone(),
        }
    }
    let weaker=weak(bound,policy,&changed);
    if kernel::implies(policy,&weaker)!=Ok(true){return None;}
    let mut judged=inv.clone();judged.policy=Some(weaker);Some(judged)
}

//! SEC4: intent quality, typed generated populations, and bounded work.
use snapback4_lang::{compile, security::{self,challenges::{self,Campaign,Reachability}}};
fn bound(source:&str)->snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let b=compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b
}
const ROLES:&str="use identity password\ntable roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('doctor','volunteer')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'\n  online only\n";
#[test]
fn equivalent_intent_is_advice_only_and_semantic_reordering_is_detected(){
    let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\ninvariant notes.read only viewer = .owner and allow\n";
    let b=bound(source);let r=security::analyze(&b);
    assert!(r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"&&n.site.line==6));
    assert!(!r.refuted());
    let b=bound(&source.replace("viewer = .owner and allow","viewer = .owner or viewer = 'service:operator'"));
    assert!(!security::analyze(&b).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"));
}
#[test]
fn per_role_population_catches_private_image_and_file_payloads_with_explicit_origin(){
    for field in ["scan: image <=5000", "scan: file <=5000 ('application/pdf')"] {
        let b=bound(&format!("{ROLES}table packages:\n  owner: principal\n  {field}\n  read <- exists roles[viewer]\n  online only\ninvariant packages.scan read only exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\nquery board(c: cursor ?):\n  return packages after c first 100 by .id\n"));
        let mut c=Campaign::default();c.generated_fixture=true;
        let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
        let roles=fixture.store().rows("roles").unwrap();
        for role in ["doctor","volunteer"] {
            assert_eq!(roles.iter().filter(|r|r.field("role").unwrap()==&snapback4_core::Value::String(role.into())).count(),2);
        }
        assert!(fixture.store().rows("packages").unwrap().len()>=2);
        challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
        let witnesses=c.violations.iter().filter(|v|v.reachability==Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        assert!(!witnesses.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
        assert!(witnesses.iter().any(|v|v.viewer.contains("volunteer")&&v.operation=="board"));
        let mut r=security::analyze(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&c);assert!(r.refuted());
        // A later synthetic witness must not replace the generated fixture provenance.
        let supplemental=challenges::run(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&supplemental);
        assert_eq!(r.invariants[0].counterexample.as_ref().unwrap().reachability,Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace);
    }
}
#[test]
fn generated_memberships_refer_to_valid_resource_and_distinct_owners(){
    let b=bound("table teams:\n  title: text 1..80\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ntable members:\n  team: teams\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('admin','member')\n  unique team, principal\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n");
    let mut c=Campaign::default();let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
    let teams=fixture.store().rows("teams").unwrap();let members=fixture.store().rows("members").unwrap();
    assert!(teams.len()>=2&&members.len()>=2,"{c:?}");
    assert_ne!(members[0].field("principal"),members[1].field("principal"));
    assert!(members.iter().all(|m|teams.iter().any(|t|t.field("id")==m.field("team"))));
}
#[test]
fn update_only_column_intent_does_not_refute_authorized_creation(){
    let b=bound("table tickets:\n  price: int\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'\n  online only\nmutation create():\n  return insert tickets {price: 1}\ninvariant tickets.price update only viewer = 'service:operator'\n");
    assert_eq!(b.invariants[0].column.as_deref(),Some("price"));
    let mut c=Campaign::default();let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
    challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
}
#[test]
fn check_exhaustion_is_explicit_and_retains_no_proof_assumptions(){
    let b=bound("table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant notes.body read only .owner = viewer\nquery notes():\n  return notes first 100 by .id\n");
    let r=security::analyze(&b);assert!(r.invariants.iter().all(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved));
    let c=challenges::run_selected_after(&b,None,Campaign::bounded(0));
    assert!(c.unsupported.iter().any(|s|s.contains("time budget exhausted")));assert!(c.violations.is_empty());
    let mut r=security::analyze(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&c);assert!(!r.refuted());
}
#[test]
fn board_advice_names_the_unscoped_branch_but_not_resource_scoped_roles(){
    let source=format!("{ROLES}table reports:\n  owner: principal\n  rating: enum('good','bad')\n  scan: image <=5000 ?\n  read <- .owner = viewer or (exists roles[viewer, role = 'volunteer'] and .rating = 'bad')\n  online only\n");
    let b=bound(&source);assert!(security::analyze(&b).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"));
    let b=bound(&source.replace(" and .rating = 'bad'"," and .owner = viewer and .rating = 'bad'"));
    assert!(!security::analyze(&b).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"));
}
#[test]
fn dominating_argument_guard_suppresses_excluded_role_and_names_actual_risk(){
    let source=format!("{ROLES}table docs:\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\n  online only\nmutation register(role: enum('doctor','volunteer')):\n  require role = 'volunteer' else NOT_ALLOWED\n  return insert roles {{principal: viewer,role}}\n").replace("insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'","insert <- .principal = viewer");
    let b=bound(&source);let mut r=security::analyze(&b);security::add_source_notes(&mut r,&[("schema.q".into(),source.clone())],&b);
    assert!(!r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION"),"{:?}",r.notes);
    let source=source.replace("  require role = 'volunteer' else NOT_ALLOWED\n","");
    let b=bound(&source);let mut r=security::analyze(&b);security::add_source_notes(&mut r,&[("schema.q".into(),source)],&b);
    assert!(r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION"&&n.message.contains("'doctor'")),"{:?}",r.notes);
}

#[test]
fn projected_media_scalar_uses_a_coupled_verified_ledger(){
    let b=bound(&format!("{ROLES}table packages:\n  scan: image <=5000\n  read <- exists roles[viewer]\n  online only\ninvariant packages.scan read only exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\nquery scan(package: packages):\n  p = packages[package] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  return p.scan\n"));

}

#[test]
fn draft_candidates_are_never_equivalent_to_the_read_gate(){
    let b=bound(&format!("{ROLES}table docs:\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\n  online only\n"));
    let draft=security::draft(&b);
    assert!(!draft.lines().any(|line|line.contains("invariant docs.body")&&line.contains("'doctor'")));
    assert!(draft.lines().any(|line|line.contains("invariant docs.body")&&line.contains("'volunteer'")));
}

#[test]
fn fixture_free_first_caller_role_claim_has_an_actual_empty_start_trace(){
    let source="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('staff','guest')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable secrets:\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'staff']\n  online only\nmutation register(role: text 1..20):\n  require role = 'staff' or role = 'guest' else BAD_ROLE\n  return insert roles {principal: viewer,role}\ninvariant roles.role write only viewer = 'service:operator'\n";
    let b=bound(source);let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::generated_campaign(&b,&mut c,false);
    assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace&&v.history.iter().any(|s|s.operation=="register")&&v.supporting_rows.values().all(Vec::is_empty)),"{c:?}");
}

#[test]
fn rejected_fallback_final_exists_world_never_becomes_a_failing_fixture(){
    let source="table roots:\n  creator: principal\n  read <- allow\n  after insert <- exists members[root = .id]\n  online only\ntable members:\n  root: roots\n  proof: image <=1\n  unique root\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ninvariant roots.read only deny\nquery roots():\n  return roots first 100 by .id\n";
    let b=bound(source);let mut c=Campaign::default();
    assert!(challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).is_err(),"invalid final-exists seed must remain unsupported");
    challenges::generated_campaign(&b,&mut c,false);
    assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
    assert!(c.unsupported.iter().any(|s|s.contains("final-exists")),"{c:?}");
}

#[test]
fn bounded_campaign_charges_initial_worlds_and_every_mutation_step(){
    let b=bound("table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nmutation add():\n  return insert notes {owner: viewer,body: 'x'}\ninvariant notes.read only .owner = viewer\nquery inspect():\n  return notes first 100 by .id\n");
    let mut c=Campaign::bounded(10000);c.work_remaining=Some(1);
    let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(c.work_remaining,Some(0));
    challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
    assert_eq!(c.attempted,0);assert!(c.unsupported.iter().any(|s|s.contains("work budget exhausted")));
    let fixture=snapback4_core::Engine::memory(b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();
    let mut c=Campaign::bounded(10000);c.work_remaining=Some(1);
    challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
    assert_eq!(c.attempted,0,"the one reservation pays for the replay world, leaving no mutation step");
}

#[test]
fn the_same_update_tuple_reaches_every_role_before_diagonal_variation(){

    let b=bound(&source);let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::generated_campaign(&b,&mut c,false);
    assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.reachability==Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace&&v.history.iter().any(|s|s.operation=="correct")),"{c:?}");
}

}

#[test]
fn inherited_expiry_replacement_cannot_refute_a_guarded_post_retirement_write(){
    use snapback4_core::{Engine,Store,Value,Context};
    use std::collections::BTreeMap;
    fn row(fields:impl IntoIterator<Item=(&'static str,Value)>)->Value{Value::Row(fields.into_iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.into(),v)).collect())}
    fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
    let b=bound("table roots:\n  until: time\n  immutable until\n  expire at .until\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ntable slots:\n  root: roots\n  immutable root\n  read through .root\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  score: int\n  read <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\ntable approvals:\n  slot: slots on delete cascade\n  doc: docs\n  unique doc\n  cap 1 by slot\n  read <- allow\n  online only\nmutation replace(slot: slots, root: roots, doc: docs):\n  insert slots {id: slot, root}\n  require not exists approvals[doc = doc] else HELD\n  update docs[doc] {score: 2}\n  return null\ninvariant docs.score update only not exists approvals[doc = .id]\n");
    let now=challenges::NOW;
    let mut fixture=Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(),b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),now-1000).unwrap();
    fixture.seed_at(vec![
        ("roots".into(),row([("id",id("old")),("until",Value::Int(now-1))])),
        ("roots".into(),row([("id",id("new")),("until",Value::Int(now+10000))])),
        ("slots".into(),row([("id",id("slot")),("root",id("old"))])),
        ("docs".into(),row([("id",id("doc")),("score",Value::Int(1))])),
        ("approvals".into(),row([("id",id("approval")),("slot",id("slot")),("doc",id("doc"))])),
    ],now-1000).unwrap();
    assert!(b.schema.tables["slots"].expire.is_none()&&b.schema.tables["slots"].is_expiring());
    let mut actual=fixture.fork_fixture().unwrap();
    let result=actual.mutate("replace",None,Context::new("dev:security-a",BTreeMap::from([("slot".into(),id("slot")),("root".into(),id("new")),("doc".into(),id("doc"))])).at(now)).unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}));
    assert!(actual.store().rows("approvals").unwrap().is_empty());
    let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
    assert!(c.operations.get("replace").copied().unwrap_or(0)>0);
    assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"the guard sees approvals after inherited retirement: {c:?}");
}

+fn role_candidates(bound:&Bound,target:&str,row:&snapback4_core::schema::Table)->Vec<RuleExpr>{

+                        Some(C::Principal)=>{principals+=1;RuleTerm::Viewer},
+                        Some(C::Ref{table}) if table==target=>RuleTerm::Field("id".into()),

+/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
+/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
+pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
+    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
+    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
+        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
+    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
+        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+}
 /// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
 /// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
 /// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
 pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
+    let first=campaign.violations.len();
+    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
     column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
     histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
+    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
+        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
+    }}
 }

+//! Deterministic role populations validated by the ordinary seed boundary.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#132-generated-initial-worlds-and-password-only-test — generated initialization is explicit.
+use super::*;
+/// No network, credentials, development doors, durable store or scratch files.
+pub fn fixture(bound:&Bound,out:&mut Campaign)->Result<Engine,String>{
+    if !out.reserve(1){return Err("generated fixture world not reserved; remaining initialization uncovered".into());}
+    let mut people=Vec::new();
+    for (table,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
+        let mut role_columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table);
+        role_columns.extend(["role","kind"].into_iter().filter(|c|t.columns.contains_key(*c)).map(str::to_owned));
+        for column in role_columns{
+            {
+                for role in super::super::privilege::role_values(bound,table,&column) {for n in 0..2 {people.push((table.clone(),column.clone(),role.clone(),format!("account:security-{table}-{role}-{n}")));}}
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    for seat in histories::viewers(bound).into_iter().filter(|p|p.starts_with("service:")) {for n in 0..2{people.push((String::new(),String::new(),String::new(),format!("account:security-{}-{n}",seat.replace(':',"-"))));}}
+    if people.is_empty(){for n in 0..2{people.push((String::new(),String::new(),String::new(),format!("account:security-owner-{n}")));}}
+    if people.len()>64{people.truncate(64);out.unsupported.insert("generated roster capped at 64 principals; additional roles remain uncovered".into());}
+    let mut grant_tables=BTreeSet::new();
+    fn grants(rule:&RuleExpr,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
+        RuleExpr::Exists{table,key,equalities,..} if key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|v|matches!(v,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>{out.insert(table.clone());},
+        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{grants(r,out)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>grants(r,out),_=>{}
+    }}
+    for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){grants(&t.rules.read,&mut grant_tables);}
+    let counts=bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(table,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()).map(|(table,t)|{
+        let own=people.iter().filter(|(roster,_,_,_)|roster==table).count();(table.clone(),if own>0{own}else if grant_tables.contains(table)||t.rules.read.any_term(&|term|matches!(term,RuleTerm::Field(c) if t.columns.get(c).is_some_and(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)))){people.len().max(1usize<<t.columns.values().filter(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)).count().min(3))}else{t.columns.values().filter_map(|k|if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){Some(variants.len())}else{None}).max().unwrap_or(2).max(1usize<<t.columns.values().filter(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)).count().min(3)).clamp(2,8)})
+    }).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
+    let mut rows=BTreeMap::new();
+    for (table,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
+        if !counts.contains_key(table){continue;}
+        let own=people.iter().filter(|(roster,_,_,_)|roster==table).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+        if t.columns.values().filter(|k|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal)).count()>3 {out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}: full Cartesian combinations above three principal columns remain uncovered"));}
+        let mut population=Vec::new();
+        for n in 0..counts[table]{
+            let person=if own.is_empty(){&people[n%people.len()]}else{own[n]};
+            let mut fields=BTreeMap::from([("id".into(),Value::Id(format!("security-{table}-{n}")))]);
+            let mut principal=0;
+            for (column,kind) in &t.columns {
+                if column=="id"||t.engine_field(column)||t.recorded.contains_key(column)||t.maintained.contains_key(column){continue;}
+                let mut value=if kind.is_optional()&&n%2==0{Value::Null}else{match sample(kind.required(),4+n){Some(value)=>value,None=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}.{column}: unsupported typed sample"));continue;}}};
+                if value==Value::Null&&kind.is_optional(){fields.insert(column.clone(),value);continue;}
+                match kind.required(){
+                    C::Principal=>{
+                        let linked=!own.is_empty()||grant_tables.contains(table)||t.constraints.iter().any(|constraint|matches!(constraint,Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{key,..} if key.iter().any(|term|matches!(term,RuleTerm::Field(c) if c==column))));
+                        value=Value::Id(if linked{if principal==0{person.3.clone()}else{people[(n+principal)%people.len()].3.clone()}}else{if (n>>principal.min(5))&1==0{VIEWER}else{OTHER}.into()});principal+=1;
+                    },
+                    C::Ref{table:target}=>{value=Value::Id(format!("security-{target}-{}",n%counts.get(target).copied().unwrap_or(1)));},
+                    C::Enum{..}|C::Text{..} if person.0==*table&&person.1==*column=>value=Value::String(person.2.clone()),
+                    C::Text{max}=>{let minimum=t.constraints.iter().filter_map(|c|if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=c{(field==column).then_some(*min as usize)}else{None}).max().unwrap_or(1);let suffix=format!("sample{n}");value=Value::String(format!("{suffix}{}","x".repeat(minimum.saturating_sub(suffix.len()))).chars().take(*max as usize).collect());},
+                    C::Time=>value=Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_deref()==Some(column){86_400_000}else{-1000}),
+                    _=>{},
+                }
+                fields.insert(column.clone(),value);
+            }
+            population.push(Value::Row(fields.into()));
+        }
+        rows.insert(table.clone(),population);
+    }
+    // Batch validation preserves final-exists, parent ordering and maintains.
+    if let Ok(engine)=engine_with(bound,&rows,Vec::new()){return Ok(engine);}
+    if !out.reserve(1){return Err("generated fallback world not reserved; initialization uncovered".into());}
+    let mut engine=engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new())?;
+    let mut pending=rows.into_iter().flat_map(|(table,rows)|rows.into_iter().map(move|row|(table.clone(),row))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+    let mut reasons=BTreeMap::new();
+    for _ in 0..=bound.schema.tables.len(){
+        let before=pending.len();let mut retry=Vec::new();
+        for (table,mut row) in pending {
+            if !out.reserve(1){validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;return Ok(engine);}
+            let attempt=(||{
+                for (column,kind) in &bound.schema.tables[&table].columns {if let Ok(value)=row.field(column){let value=hydrate(&mut engine,kind,value)?;if let Value::Row(fields)=&mut row{fields.insert(column.clone(),value);}}}
+                engine.seed_at([(table.clone(),row.clone())],NOW).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;Ok::<_,String>(())
+            })();
+            if let Err(why)=attempt{reasons.insert(table.clone(),why);retry.push((table,row));}
+        }
+        pending=retry;if pending.len()==before{break;}
+    }
+    for (table,why) in reasons{if pending.iter().any(|(name,_)|name==&table){out.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture {table}: some related rows declined by ordinary validation: {why}"));}}
+    validate_fixture(bound,&mut engine)?;
+    Ok(engine)
+}

+thread_local! {

+/// Only exact kernel implication results are reused, never concrete judgments.
 pub fn implies(admission: &R, policy: &R) -> Result<bool,String> {
+    let key=serde_json::to_string(&(admission,policy)).unwrap();
+    if let Some(result)=IMPLICATIONS.with(|cache|cache.borrow().get(&key).cloned()){return result;}
+    let result=uncached(admission,policy);
+    IMPLICATIONS.with(|cache|{let mut cache=cache.borrow_mut();if cache.len()>=4096{cache.clear();}cache.insert(key,result.clone());});
+    result
+}

+fn argument_guard(expr:&Expr,argument:&str,target:&str)->Option<bool>{
+    match expr {
+        Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="="||op=="!="=>{
+            let pair=match (left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()){
+                (Expr::Name{name,..},Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str(value),..})|(Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str(value),..},Expr::Name{name,..}) if name==argument=>Some(value),_=>None,
+            }?;Some((pair==target)==(op=="="))
+        },
+        Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="and"||op=="or"=>{
+            let (a,b)=(argument_guard(left,argument,target),argument_guard(right,argument,target));
+            if op=="and"{if a==Some(false)||b==Some(false){Some(false)}else if a==Some(true)&&b==Some(true){Some(true)}else{None}}
+            else if a==Some(true)||b==Some(true){Some(true)}else if a==Some(false)&&b==Some(false){Some(false)}else{None}
+        },Expr::Unary{op,value,..} if op=="not"=>argument_guard(value,argument,target).map(|v|!v),_=>None,
+    }
+}

fn equivalent_intent_is_advice_only_and_semantic_reordering_is_detected(){
    let source="table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\ninvariant notes.read only viewer = .owner and allow\n";

    assert!(r.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"&&n.site.line==6));
    assert!(!r.refuted());
    let b=bound(&source.replace("viewer = .owner and allow","viewer = .owner or viewer = 'service:operator'"));

#[test]
fn per_role_population_catches_private_image_and_file_payloads_with_explicit_origin(){
    for field in ["scan: image <=5000", "scan: file <=5000 ('application/pdf')"] {
        let b=bound(&format!("{ROLES}table packages:\n  owner: principal\n  {field}\n  read <- exists roles[viewer]\n  online only\ninvariant packages.scan read only exists roles[viewer, role = 'doctor']\nquery board(c: cursor ?):\n  return packages after c first 100 by .id\n"));
        let mut c=Campaign::default();c.generated_fixture=true;
        let fixture=challenges::generated_fixture(&b,&mut c).unwrap();
        let roles=fixture.store().rows("roles").unwrap();
        for role in ["doctor","volunteer"] {
            assert_eq!(roles.iter().filter(|r|r.field("role").unwrap()==&snapback4_core::Value::String(role.into())).count(),2);
        }
        assert!(fixture.store().rows("packages").unwrap().len()>=2);
        challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
        let witnesses=c.violations.iter().filter(|v|v.reachability==Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        assert!(!witnesses.is_empty(),"{c:?}");
        assert!(witnesses.iter().any(|v|v.viewer.contains("volunteer")&&v.operation=="board"));
        let mut r=security::analyze(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&c);assert!(r.refuted());
        // A later synthetic witness must not replace the generated fixture provenance.
        let supplemental=challenges::run(&b);challenges::apply(&mut r,&supplemental);
        assert_eq!(r.invariants[0].counterexample.as_ref().unwrap().reachability,Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace);
    }
}

#[test]
fn inherited_expiry_replacement_cannot_refute_a_guarded_post_retirement_write(){
    use snapback4_core::{Engine,Store,Value,Context};
    use std::collections::BTreeMap;
    fn row(fields:impl IntoIterator<Item=(&'static str,Value)>)->Value{Value::Row(fields.into_iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.into(),v)).collect())}
    fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
    let b=bound("table roots:\n  until: time\n  immutable until\n  expire at .until\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ntable slots:\n  root: roots\n  immutable root\n  read through .root\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  score: int\n  read <- allow\n  update <- allow\n  online only\ntable approvals:\n  slot: slots on delete cascade\n  doc: docs\n  unique doc\n  cap 1 by slot\n  read <- allow\n  online only\nmutation replace(slot: slots, root: roots, doc: docs):\n  insert slots {id: slot, root}\n  require not exists approvals[doc = doc] else HELD\n  update docs[doc] {score: 2}\n  return null\ninvariant docs.score update only not exists approvals[doc = .id]\n");
    let now=challenges::NOW;
    let mut fixture=Engine::new_at(Store::memory().unwrap(),b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone()),now-1000).unwrap();
    fixture.seed_at(vec![
        ("roots".into(),row([("id",id("old")),("until",Value::Int(now-1))])),
        ("roots".into(),row([("id",id("new")),("until",Value::Int(now+10000))])),
        ("slots".into(),row([("id",id("slot")),("root",id("old"))])),
        ("docs".into(),row([("id",id("doc")),("score",Value::Int(1))])),
        ("approvals".into(),row([("id",id("approval")),("slot",id("slot")),("doc",id("doc"))])),
    ],now-1000).unwrap();
    assert!(b.schema.tables["slots"].expire.is_none()&&b.schema.tables["slots"].is_expiring());
    let mut actual=fixture.fork_fixture().unwrap();
    let result=actual.mutate("replace",None,Context::new("dev:security-a",BTreeMap::from([("slot".into(),id("slot")),("root".into(),id("new")),("doc".into(),id("doc"))])).at(now)).unwrap();
    assert!(matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Sent{..}));
    assert!(actual.store().rows("approvals").unwrap().is_empty());
    let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&fixture,&mut c);
    assert!(c.operations.get("replace").copied().unwrap_or(0)>0);
    assert!(c.violations.is_empty(),"the guard sees approvals after inherited retirement: {c:?}");
}

//! Referential actions on delete (LLP 3000.039 §3): before a delete removes
//! its row, reserve a schema-fixed charge, close over the `cascade`
//! references, refuse while a `restrict` reference still holds a closure row
//! from outside the closure, clear `set null` references outside it, and
//! remove the closure children first. The caller then removes the row itself.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — closure first; probes exclude the closure; deletion dominates set null.
use crate::engine::runner::Runner;
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, references::{OnDelete, Referencing}},
    value::Value,
};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.reference_actions(table_name, old, false)
    }

    // Recovery has no later root-delete hook to preflight physical read-through
    // retention. Include that root before the first child action can write.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn apply_missing_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, identity: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.reference_actions(table_name, identity, true)
    }

    fn reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value, missing_root: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        if !schema.is_referenced(table_name) { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(table_name) else {
            // A device without a reached child's exported price cannot charge
            // what the server charges; the server always has every price.
            if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} needs every referencing table's write price; queued without a local delete"))); }
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name}'s delete actions have no reservation: a cascading reference lacks its cap or its child's write price (guide writes)")));
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.2 — what the commit publishes is fixed by the schema, not by the rows the actions reach.
        let closure = schema.action_tables(table_name);
        // A public device projection carries the private column's price but
        // not the maintain that computes its value. If an action can write
        // such a source, settlement must supply the authoritative mirror.
        // @ref LLP 3000.007.001#3.7 — devices store maintained columns but never derive them.
        if self.device && closure.written.iter().any(|source| schema.write_reservation(source)
            .is_some_and(|plan| plan.price(crate::schema::reservations::COLUMNS)[3] != 0)) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} can change a server-maintained column; queued without a local delete")));
        }
        for table in &closure.written {
            if schema.visible(table) { self.written_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
            self.action_tables.insert(table.clone());
        }
        for table in &closure.read { self.read_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
        if !closure.written.is_empty() { self.tx.widen_change_evidence(); }
        for (kind, amount) in [(MeterKind::Steps, price[0]), (MeterKind::IndexEntries, price[1]), (MeterKind::Examined, price[2]), (MeterKind::RowsWritten, price[3]), (MeterKind::OutputBytes, price[5]), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, price[6])] {
            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }
        }
        // Pending event images survive the closure and subsequent statements.
        // Keep their schema-fixed reservation until the request ends.
        let events = schema.delete_event_heap(table_name).unwrap_or(0);
        self.meter.heap(events)?;
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(price[4].saturating_sub(events))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — draw the reservation's rows from the request's physical allowance before the first seek.
        if !self.context.is_system() { self.tx.draw(table_name, price[2])?; }
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort));
        // The fixed action reservation prepays the closure itself, but held-
        // group and authority proofs remain device-only bounded work. Keep the
        // active allowance reachable by nested `device_work` calls while the
        // closure runs under its prepaid unbounded meter. Recursive cascade
        // removals see the already-stored allowance and must not take it over.
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — every device-only proof spends the separate bounded physical-work allowance.
        let owns_device_allowance = self.device_work_active && self.device_allowance.is_none();
        let mut meter = Some(meter);
        if owns_device_allowance { self.device_allowance = meter.take(); }
        let prepaid = self.tx.reference_prepaid(true);
        let action = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
        let result = self.reference_closure(table_name, old, missing_root);
        self.engine_action = action;
        self.tx.reference_prepaid(prepaid);
        self.meter = if owns_device_allowance {
            self.device_allowance.take().expect("active device allowance")
        } else {
            meter.expect("prepaid action meter")
        };
        self.meter.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }

    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value, missing_root: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
        let mut next = 0;
        while next < visits.len() {
            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
            next += 1;
            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
                if self.device && !through_reference(&r) { self.prove_reference_held(&r, &id)?; }
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                self.each_child(&r, &id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    if seen.insert((r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned())) { visits.push((r.child.to_owned(), child)); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
            }
        }
        // 4. Restrict: every probe runs, streaming past closure rows (the root
        // included) to the first outside witness; the first occupied reference
        // in schema order is reported. One reference's probes visit each closure
        // row at most once, plus one witness per probe (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
        let mut occupied: Option<(String, String, String)> = None;
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Restrict || through_reference(&r) { continue; }
                let mut outside = false;
                self.each_child(&r, id, false, &mut |_, child| {
                    outside = !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned()));
                    Ok(!outside)
                })?;
                let candidate = (r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned(), table.clone());
                if outside && occupied.as_ref().is_none_or(|o| candidate < *o) { occupied = Some(candidate); }
            }
        }
        if let Some((child, column, parent)) = occupied {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            self.refusing_clause(&child, &format!("{column}: {parent} (on delete restrict)"));
            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
        }
        // A cascaded row can itself have a physically retained read-through
        // child. Decide that blocker before set-null writes begin, so expiry
        // can retain this root and continue without relying on transaction
        // rollback to undo a partial closure.
        for (table, id) in visits.iter().skip(if missing_root { 0 } else { 1 }) {
            if self.physical_children_outside(table, id, &seen)? {
                return Err(self.parent_has_children(table));
            }
        }
        // 5. Set null outside the closure. Each group is gathered whole before
        // any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling that shares the
        // reference's index and move it behind a page cursor. Each row is then
        // reread current before its write.
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — gather, then clear: no row moves out of a walk it has not finished.
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::SetNull { continue; }
                let mut group: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
                self.each_child(&r, id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    let child = child.as_str()?.to_owned();
                    if !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.clone())) { group.push(child); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
                for child in group {
                    let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, r.child, &child))?;
                    if let Some(current) = current.filter(|row| row.field(r.column).is_ok_and(|v| v == id)) {
                        self.clear_reference(r.child, r.column, current)?;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // 6. Delete the closure, children first, each from its current image.
        let reached: Vec<(String, String)> = visits[1..].iter().map(|(t, id)| Ok((t.clone(), id.as_str()?.to_owned()))).collect::<Result<_, RuntimeError>>()?;
        drop(visits);
        for (table, id) in deletion_order(schema, &reached) {
            let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &table, &id))?;
            let Some(current) = current else { continue };
            self.delete_reached(&table, current)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
        } else {
            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
        };
        let mut live = 0_u64;
        for (prefix, gt) in ranges {
            let mut bounds = Bounds { gt, ..Default::default() };
            loop {
                let page = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.scan(allowance, r.child, &index, &prefix, &bounds, Dir::Asc, page_rows))?;
                let more = page.len() == page_rows;
                if let Some((_, key)) = page.last() { bounds.after = crate::key::decode(key).map(|v| v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect()); }
                let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(page.len());
                for (row, _) in page {
                    if self.device {
                        // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — a device predicts only over current rows: retained history queues the delete, and only a live, current (or own provisional) row is a witness.
                        // A retained, no longer current row is work the reservation does not
                        // price and a witness the device cannot judge: the delete queues.
                        if self.tx.retained_row(r.child, &row)? && !self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? {
                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting meets retained {} history that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
                        }
                        if self.expired(r.table, &row) || !(self.tx.current_row(r.child, &row)? || self.is_provisional(r.child, &row)?) {
                            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs a current image of every {} row that references it; queued without a local delete", r.child)));
                        }
                    } else if self.expired(r.table, &row) { continue; }
                    live += 1;
                    // Activation validates caps and every write keeps them; more than the cap
                    // is a store that broke its own invariant, never a hidden-row count.
                    if live > cap { return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} holds more rows than its cap for one {} row", r.child, r.column, r.column))); }
                    ids.push(row.field("id")?.clone());
                }
                for child in ids {
                    if !visit(self, child)? { return Ok(()); }
                }
                if !more { break; }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// A device predicts only over a referencing group it holds completely.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.4 — the held-group proof of LLP 3000.007 §20.5, per closure row and reference.
    fn prove_reference_held(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let index = self.schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
        let (child, key) = (r.child.to_owned(), [id.clone()]);
        // Key-decided readability, and the group itself held (a rejoined or
        // unacquired range is unknown even when the rule admits the viewer).
        if self.device_work(|runner| runner.upsert_absence_proven(&child, &index, &key))? && self.tx.current_group(&child, &index, &key)? { return Ok(()); }
        Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting needs every {} row that references it held; queued without a local delete", r.child)))
    }

    /// The engine's own write of a `set null` reference: no rule, every constraint.
    fn clear_reference(&mut self, table: &str, column: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let mut new = old.clone();
        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut new { fields.insert(column.to_owned(), Value::Null); }
        let patch = crate::value::object([(column, Value::Null)]);
        let outer = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
        let result = self.target_attempt(table, |runner| {
            runner.reserve_maintains(table, &runner.schema.tables[table])?;
            runner.replace_authorized(table, old, new, true, &patch, 0)
        });
        self.engine_action = outer;
        result
    }

    /// The engine's own delete of a closure row: no rule, its constraints, its
    /// `read through` children's probe, maintains and partition events.
    fn delete_reached(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            runner.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
            let table = &runner.schema.tables[table_name];
            runner.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
            runner.delete_row(table_name, old)
        })
    }
}

/// Rows a child scan holds per seek: the reservation's transient `4 × row`
/// (LLP 3000.039 §5.1, `Schema::delete_reservation`).
const PAGE: usize = crate::schema::references::REFERENCE_PAGE;

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, rows::{MemoryRows, Rows}, value::{Value, object}};

    #[test]
    fn prepaid_actions_keep_nested_device_proofs_bounded() {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("nested-allowance.q".into(), r#"
table parents:
  public
table children:
  parent: parents on delete cascade
  cap 1 by parent
  public
"#.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let schema: crate::Schema = crate::json_codec::from_text(
            &crate::json_codec::to_text(&bound.schema).unwrap(),
        ).unwrap();
        let rows = MemoryRows::new();
        let parent = object([("id", Value::Id("p".into()))]);
        rows.put("parents", &schema.tables["parents"], &parent, None).unwrap();
        rows.put("children", &schema.tables["children"], &object([
            ("id", Value::Id("c".into())), ("parent", Value::Id("p".into())),
        ]), None).unwrap();
        let price = schema.delete_reservation("parents").unwrap();
        let ceilings = Ceilings {
            steps: price[0], index_entries: price[1], examined: price[2],
            rows_written: price[3], heap_bytes: price[4], output_bytes: price[5],
            rule_probes: price[6],
        };
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows, &schema, Context::system().at(1), Meter::new(ceilings, Cut::Abort), 0);
        runner.device = true;
        // Model the commit-end walk after its outer device_work swap: the
        // bounded allowance is the active meter and no spare copy exists.
        runner.device_work_active = true;
        let error = runner.apply_reference_actions("parents", &parent).unwrap_err();
        assert!(matches!(&error, RuntimeError::Prediction(message) if message.contains("allowance exhausted")), "{error:?}");
        assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.steps, price[0] + 1, "the nested proof attempted its bounded step");
    }
}

fn through_reference(r: &Referencing<'_>) -> bool {
    r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column)
}

/// The closure's rows, children before parents: a table goes first when no
/// other reached table must be removed before it (the cascade and `read
/// through` edges are acyclic by `check`), and within a table, later
/// discoveries first.
fn deletion_order(schema: &crate::schema::Schema, reached: &[(String, String)]) -> Vec<(String, String)> {
    let tables: Vec<&str> = {
        let mut seen = Vec::new();
        for (table, _) in reached { if !seen.contains(&table.as_str()) { seen.push(table.as_str()); } }
        seen
    };
    // edges: child table -> the reached tables it must be removed before
    // (its cascade parents and its `read through` parent; LLP 3000.039 §3).
    let mut edges: Vec<(&str, &str)> = Vec::new();
    for &child in &tables {
        for &parent in &tables {
            if child != parent && schema.deletion_precedes(child, parent) { edges.push((child, parent)); }
        }
    }
    let mut order: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
    let mut remaining = tables.clone();
    while !remaining.is_empty() {
        // A table no remaining table references can go now (its referencers are gone).
        let position = remaining.iter().position(|t| !remaining.iter().any(|other| other != t && edges.contains(&(*other, *t))))
            .unwrap_or(remaining.len() - 1);
        order.push(remaining.remove(position));
    }
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(reached.len());
    for table in order {
        out.extend(reached.iter().rev().filter(|(t, _)| t == table).cloned());
    }
    out
}

# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts and five actionable notes; `--verbose` retains every reason.
A mirror invariant cannot catch a wrong rule: the advisory asks
for the spec's audience, resource relationship and lifecycle condition instead.
A legitimate simple owner policy can be equivalent; the advisory never refuses it.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: it offers unconfirmed
per-role/per-column alternatives and questions, never auto-accepted policy.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE means intent and the effective rule admit the same cases
in the bounded predicate kernel. Check the original requirement before rewriting; an equivalent policy
may be correct, but cannot detect a mistake copied into both declarations.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ means a role-wide branch gated by a row value exposes every column
on matching rows.
Use a board/projection table for a public rating; declare intent for the private
notes, principals, images and files separately.

//! Lifetime-bound rule inputs, distinct from historical recorded attribution.
// @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — captures have their parent's type and activation lifecycle.
use super::*;

pub const PARENT_DEADLINE: &str = "$parentDeadline";
pub const DEADLINE: &str = "$deadline";
pub const CHILDREN: &str = "$children";
pub const LIVE_CHILDREN: &str = "$liveChildren";

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Through {
    /// Bounded live maintenance; absence preserves captured wire bytes.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub live: Option<u32>,
    pub reference: String,
    pub parent: String,
    /// Child engine field -> immediate parent field (including its captures).
    pub fields: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub identity: String,
    pub expiring: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn engine_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        (self.ephemeral.is_some() && field == DEADLINE) || self.recorded.contains_key(field)
            || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| {
                t.fields.contains_key(field) || matches!(field, DEADLINE | PARENT_DEADLINE)
            })
    }
    /// One classification for every expiry gate, including inherited deadlines.
    pub fn is_expiring(&self) -> bool {
        self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.expire.is_some() || self.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring)
    }
    pub fn expiry_column(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        if self.ephemeral.is_some() || self.through.is_some() && self.is_expiring() {
            Some(DEADLINE)
        } else {
            self.expire.as_deref()
        }
    }
    pub fn deadline(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<i64> {
        match self
            .expiry_column()
            .and_then(|field| row_image(row, self).field(field).ok().cloned())
        {
            Some(Value::Int(at)) => Some(at),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    /// Never trust a restored effective instant; recompute from immutable capture.
    pub fn recompute_deadline(&self, row: &mut Value) -> Result<(), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
        if self.through.is_none() {
            return Ok(());
        }
        let authored = self
            .expire
            .as_ref()
            .and_then(|c| match row_image(row, self).field(c) {
                Ok(Value::Int(at)) => Some(*at),
                _ => None,
            });
        let parent = match row.field(PARENT_DEADLINE)? {
            Value::Int(at) => Some(*at),
            Value::Null => None,
            _ => {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Input(
                    "captured parent deadline must be a time or null".into(),
                ));
            }
        };
        let deadline = authored
            .into_iter()
            .chain(parent)
            .min()
            .map(Value::Int)
            .unwrap_or(Value::Null);
        if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = row {
            fields.insert(DEADLINE.into(), deadline);
        }
        Ok(())

fn prior_policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,prior:&mut Option<Engine>)->Result<bool,String>{
    match prior.as_mut(){Some(e)=>policy_in(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,e),None=>policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row)}
}
fn assertions(bound:&Bound,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="always"){
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
            if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)!=Ok(false){continue;}
            // A pre-existing seed defect is supplemental; a newly violated
            // assertion or changed image is an authored committed observation.
            if old!=Some(row)||old.is_some_and(|r|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(r),r,prior)==Ok(true)){
                violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,"always");
            }
        }
    }
}
fn changed(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    assertions(bound,before,after,initial,viewer,steps,prior,current,out);
    if !super::super::census::body(&p.body){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: loop/opaque write boundary requires executed-effect tracing",p.name));return;}
    for inv in &bound.invariants{
        if matches!(inv.target.as_str(),"read"|"always")||inv.operation.as_ref().is_some_and(|op|op!=&p.name){continue;}
        let judged;
        let inv=if inv.target=="always"{inv}else{
            let Some(stable)=stability::necessary(bound,p,inv)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}: unknown/implicit policy changes need executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.target_label()));continue;};
            judged=stable;&judged
        };
        // A net image cannot identify an intermediate write or implicit effect.
        let writes=ir::write_tables(p).into_iter().filter(|(t,_)|t==&inv.table).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if writes.len()!=1 {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}.{}: multiple or implicit writes require executed-effect tracing",p.name,inv.table,inv.target));continue;}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1!="delete"&&before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|old|!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: commit-end/implicit retirement is not an executed delete",p.name));}
        if (inv.target=="delete"||inv.operation.is_some())&&writes[0].1=="delete"{
            for old in before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().filter(|old|!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(old,NOW)&&!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|r|r.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(t,_)|deps.contains(t)&&t!=&inv.table){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: delete policy dependency changes before disclosure",p.name));continue;}
                if prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(old),old,prior)==Ok(false){violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,old,Some(old),initial,"delete");}
            }
        }
        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok()&&!bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].expired(r,NOW));
            if old==Some(row){continue;}
            let effect=if old.is_some(){"update"}else{"insert"};
            if !super::super::effects(inv).contains(&effect){continue;}
            if inv.column.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|old.is_some_and(|o|o.field(c).ok()==row.field(c).ok())){continue;}
            if inv.target=="always" {
                if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)==Ok(false){violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,effect);}
            }else{
                let deps=inv.policy.as_ref().map(kernel::dependencies).unwrap_or_default();
                if ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(t,_)|deps.contains(t)&&t!=&inv.table) {out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: write policy dependency changes within the operation; tracing required",p.name));continue;}
                if prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,old,row,prior)==Ok(false)||old.is_some_and(|old|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(old),old,prior)==Ok(false)){
                    violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,effect);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
fn role_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut groups=Vec::new();
    let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
    if !ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(table,_)|!super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table).is_empty()){return out;}
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return Vec::new();}
    let Ok(mut judge)=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())else{return Vec::new();};
    for (table,_) in ir::write_tables(p){
        let columns=super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,&table);
        if !columns.iter().any(|c|json.contains(&format!("\"{c}\":"))){continue;}
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&table];
        for row in initial.get(&table).into_iter().flatten(){
            let principal=t.columns.iter().find_map(|(c,k)|matches!(k.required(),C::Principal).then(||row.field(c).ok()).flatten());
            let Some(principal)=principal else{continue;};
            let mut targets=Vec::new();
            for case in cases{
                let mut args=case.clone();
                for (name,kind) in &p.args{match kind{
                    ir::ArgType::Principal=>{args.insert(name.clone(),principal.clone());},
                    ir::ArgType::Ref{table:target} if target==&table=>{if let Ok(id)=row.field("id"){args.insert(name.clone(),id.clone());}},_=>{}
                }}
                if !targets.contains(&args){targets.push(args);}
            }
            let mut row_cases=Vec::new();
            for actor in actors{
                let owner=columns.iter().filter_map(|c|t.written_by.get(c)).any(|a|row.field(&a.owner).ok()==Some(&Value::Id(actor.clone())));
                if !campaign.reserve(1){return out;}
                let gate=judge.fixture_policy(&t.update_rule_judged(),actor,Some(row),row,NOW).ok();
                if !owner&&gate!=Some(rules::Verdict::Readable){continue;}
                for args in &targets{row_cases.push((p,actor.clone(),args.clone()));}
            }
            groups.push(row_cases);

+/// Fixture-free campaigns retain empty-start bootstraps and populated role worlds.
+/// The two initial states share the existing 512-single/512-pair work envelope.
+pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
+    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
+    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
+        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
+    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
+        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
+        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
+    }
+    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
+}
 /// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
 /// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
 /// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
 pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
+    let first=campaign.violations.len();
+    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
     column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
     histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
+    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
+        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
+    }}
 }
